<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Minionese: Multilingual LLM Safety Benchmark & Mechanistic Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10112) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced Minionese, a multilingual jailbreak benchmark covering 18 languages, 4 resource tiers, and 4 perturbation types, paired with a geometric mechanistic analysis revealing that low-resource jailbreaks succeed by routing harmful content through a geometrically misaligned subspace that fails to trigger refusal directions. This work demonstrates that English-only safety evaluations are insufficient, as prompts reliably refused in English can elicit harmful compliance in non-English and low-resource settings, highlighting the need for multilingual safety alignment that accounts for script family, perturbation type, and per-language coverage. The benchmark includes 4 perturbation types: standard translation, code-switching, transliteration, and translationese, and reveals that transliteration vulnerability is mediated by script identity, while code-switching maintains effectiveness through the lowest-resource tier, with a sharp safety regime transition between Tiers 2 and 3 consistent across models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are safety-aligned to refuse harmful requests, but this alignment often fails for non-English inputs. Prior work found that low-resource languages produce unsafe outputs at roughly three times the rate of high-resource languages. The geometry of refusal in LLMs involves a refusal direction in activation space; jailbreak attacks can bypass this by routing harmful content away from that direction.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Indirect Data Poisoning Could Industrialize Scientific Fraud](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10712) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced and empirically demonstrated a new attack called indirect data poisoning, where adversaries corrupt open datasets to turn honest AI-assisted scientists into unwitting distributors of scientific fraud at scale. This attack could industrialize scientific fraud, undermining trust in AI-assisted research across critical domains like hiring discrimination and autonomous vehicle safety, with a 49.56% success rate and only 6.0% detection rate. The attack was tested on five socially-salient topics using three frontier AI systems (Claude Code with Claude Opus 4.7, Codex with GPT-5.5, Gemini CLI with Gemini 3.1 Pro) across 450 ethically contained runs, requiring no trigger words or fabricated papers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Data poisoning is a type of adversarial attack where malicious data is introduced into a model's training or retrieval pipeline to corrupt its behavior. Indirect data poisoning specifically targets open datasets that autonomous research agents may retrieve and use, turning honest scientists into unwitting accomplices. This paper highlights a new vulnerability in the AI-assisted scientific research ecosystem.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [LLMs Show Epistemic Paternalism in History Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11292) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A systematic audit of four LLMs as history tutors reveals four patterns of epistemic paternalism, including differential refusal blocking 76.7% of educational requests from low-tier students and a 3× reduction in access to geopolitical complexity for marginalized learners. This research exposes systemic epistemic injustice in LLM-mediated education, showing how safety alignment can act as a paternalistic filter that perpetuates narrative segregation and hermeneutical injustice, with high impact on AI ethics, fairness, and educational equity. The study evaluated 1,800 responses about the 1989 Romanian Revolution across five student personas varying by ethnicity and socio-economic tier, using four LLMs including LLaMA, which produced a 5× higher victimization-to-politics vocabulary ratio for Roma students compared to elite peers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Epistemic paternalism refers to epistemic authorities interfering with others' epistemic activities without consultation, ostensibly to make them epistemically better off. In AI, safety alignment can lead to differential refusal, where models refuse to answer certain queries based on user demographics, and epistemic gatekeeping, where access to complex knowledge is restricted. This study builds on Fricker's concept of hermeneutical injustice, where marginalized groups are denied the interpretive resources to understand their own experiences.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [First Model-Free Universal AI Agent Proven Optimal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23242) ⭐️ 9.0/10

The paper introduces AIQI (Universal AI with Q-Induction), the first model-free reinforcement learning agent proven to be asymptotically ε-optimal in general reinforcement learning. AIQI performs universal induction over distributional action-value functions instead of environment models. This theoretical breakthrough expands the diversity of known universal agents, which were previously all model-based like AIXI. It could influence future AI research by providing a new paradigm for building optimal agents without explicit environment models. AIQI is proven strong asymptotically ε-optimal and asymptotically ε-Bayes-optimal under a grain of truth condition. The novel proof techniques are also applied to show asymptotic ε-optimality of Self-AIXI without ad-hoc assumptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: General reinforcement learning aims to create agents that perform optimally in any environment. Previous optimal agents like AIXI are model-based, meaning they explicitly maintain and update a model of the environment. Model-free agents, by contrast, learn directly from experience without building an explicit model. AIQI is the first model-free agent with proven optimality guarantees in this general setting.

## [GrandCode: AI Beats All Humans in Live Coding Contests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02721) ⭐️ 9.0/10

GrandCode, a multi-agent reinforcement learning system, achieved first place in three consecutive Codeforces live rounds (1087, 1088, 1089) in March 2026, outperforming all human participants including legendary grandmasters. This is the first AI system to consistently beat all humans in live competitive programming, marking a milestone where AI surpasses the strongest human programmers on the most challenging coding tasks. GrandCode uses a multi-agent architecture with modules for hypothesis proposal, solving, test generation, and summarization, jointly improved via post-training and online test-time RL. It introduces Agentic GRPO to handle delayed rewards and off-policy drift in multi-stage agent rollouts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Competitive programming is a human stronghold where AI has historically lagged behind top humans. Previous best systems like Gemini 3 Deep Think only achieved 8th place under non-live conditions. GrandCode's multi-agent RL approach and Agentic GRPO algorithm address key challenges in agentic reinforcement learning, enabling superior performance.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [Safety from Honesty in a Disinterested AI Predictor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29657) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Yoshua Bengio and collaborators propose a formal safety argument for a Scientist AI Predictor trained to approximate Bayesian posteriors, aiming to achieve honest predictions without goal-directed agency through epistemic contextualization and posterior-seeking training. This work addresses the critical problem of implicit agency in AI systems, offering a theoretical framework that could guide the design of safer AI predictors by ensuring honesty without goal-directed behavior. The safety argument relies on epistemic contextualization to distinguish factual claims from communication acts, and a posterior-seeking training objective that avoids using downstream effects as reward signals, proving that dangerous predictors are rare under certain assumptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: AI systems trained to optimize for outcomes can develop implicit agency—goal-directed behavior not specified by designers. Bayesian inference provides a principled way to update beliefs based on evidence, and the Scientist AI Predictor is designed to approximate the Bayesian posterior without becoming an agent itself.

**Tags**: `#AI safety`, `#Bayesian inference`, `#epistemic contextualization`, `#agency`, `#Yoshua Bengio`

## [FARS: Fully Automated AI Research System Produces 166 Papers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31651) ⭐️ 9.0/10

FARS, a fully automated AI research system, autonomously generated and advanced 166 complete research papers across 67 AI/ML topics, with all intermediate artifacts preserved as an auditable corpus. This represents a paradigm shift in automated research, demonstrating that AI can conduct large-scale, end-to-end scientific discovery without human intervention, potentially accelerating AI research methodology. The system uses stage-specific agents coordinated through a shared workspace for ideation, planning, experimentation, and writing. 282 structured reviews from volunteer reviewers covering 140 papers revealed both strong artifacts and recurring failure modes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Recent automated research systems have shown that language-model agents can generate hypotheses, run experiments, and write manuscripts, but most evidence comes from selected examples or predefined tasks. FARS is the first system to operate fully autonomously across diverse topics at scale, producing an auditable corpus rather than a curated set of successes.

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [CUDA-L2: RL-optimized HGEMM kernels beat cuBLAS by 22%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02551) ⭐️ 9.0/10

CUDA-L2 is a system that uses large language models and reinforcement learning to automatically optimize half-precision general matrix multiply (HGEMM) CUDA kernels, achieving up to 22% speedup over torch.matmul and 19.2% over cuBLAS in offline mode. This work demonstrates that even highly optimized GPU kernels like HGEMM can be further improved through LLM-guided reinforcement learning, potentially reducing reliance on hand-tuned libraries and enabling automatic performance optimization across many GPU workloads. CUDA-L2 explores 1,000 kernel configurations using CUDA execution speed as the RL reward, and in server mode (simulating real-time inference) the speedups increase further: +28.7% over torch.matmul and +26.0% over cuBLAS. The project is open-source at github.com/deepreinforce-ai/CUDA-L2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: cuBLAS is NVIDIA's proprietary library for GPU-accelerated linear algebra, heavily optimized for matrix multiplication. HGEMM (half-precision general matrix multiply) is a key operation in deep learning inference and training. Traditional kernel optimization requires extensive manual tuning or heuristic-based auto-tuning, which CUDA-L2 replaces with reinforcement learning guided by large language models.

## [Strategic Evolution Theory Unifies Game Theory and Self-Reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07901) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper synthesizes von Neumann's game theory and self-reproducing automata into a unified Theory of Strategic Evolution, introducing Games with Endogenous Players (GEPs) and Evolutionarily Stable Distributions of Intelligence (ESDIs). This framework provides a mathematical foundation for analyzing strategic replicators like AI agents and firms, with implications for AI safety, market dynamics, and institutional design. The paper proves an Alignment Impossibility Theorem showing that unrestricted self-modification destroys dynamical structure, and establishes that stable alignment requires bounded modification classes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Von Neumann founded both game theory and the theory of self-reproducing automata, but these fields remained separate. This work merges them by modeling strategic replicators—entities that optimize under constraints and reproduce—using a hierarchy of strategic layers linked by cross-level gain matrices.

<a id="item-10"></a>
## [GenAI Reduces Study Time, Hurts Learning Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21629) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A large-scale study analyzing 3.2 million learning interactions over ten years found that generative AI reduced study time on math problems by up to 31.3% among high school students and 26.9% among college students after ChatGPT's release, with a 25% decline in odds of correct response on proctored retention items. This provides the first large-scale behavioral evidence that generative AI is altering how students study and the knowledge they build, with direct implications for educational research, assessment governance, and AI policy. The study used a quasi-experimental design comparing text-based word problems (more AI-susceptible) to interactive graph-based problems (less AI-susceptible), and found that the reduction in study time vanished under proctoring, ruling out efficiency gains as the cause.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI tools like ChatGPT can solve math problems instantly, raising concerns that students may use them to bypass learning. Prior self-report surveys showed little change, while small-scale studies lacked the scale to measure learning consequences. This study leverages a ten-year panel of 3.2 million learning interactions from the ALEKS platform, combined with placement-assessment data, to provide robust evidence.

**Tags**: `#generative AI`, `#education`, `#learning outcomes`, `#time-on-task`, `#quasi-experimental`

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [Vision Encoders Share a Universal Geometric Invariant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07882) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study reveals that thirteen modern vision encoders, despite different training objectives, converge to the same 16-dimensional geometric invariant called the cross-architecture substrate. This invariant transfers across multiple visual domains and survives calibration. This finding challenges the assumption that different training objectives lead to fundamentally different internal representations, suggesting a universal structure in learned visual features. It could impact model interpretability, transfer learning, and understanding of representation learning. The substrate was identified using PCA, CKA, and Pang calibration, achieving median Procrustes-CKA of 0.679 across four domains and 0.604 across eight domains. It emerges within the first 10% of training and is not explained by pixel statistics, Gabor features, or random projections.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Vision encoders are neural networks that convert images into feature representations for tasks like classification, segmentation, or image-text matching. Researchers often assume that different training objectives produce distinct representations, but this work shows a shared geometric structure persists across architectures and domains.

**Tags**: `#vision encoders`, `#representation learning`, `#geometric invariance`, `#PCA`, `#CKA`

Researchers released Embodied-R1.5, an 8B-parameter embodied foundation model that achieves state-of-the-art on 16 out of 24 embodied VLM benchmarks, surpassing models like Gemini-Robotics-ER-1.5 and GPT-5.4. ## [Soofi S 30B-A3B: Open-Source German-English Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09424) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers released Soofi S 30B-A3B, a sovereign open-source Mixture-of-Experts hybrid Mamba Transformer foundation model for German and English, pretrained on 27 trillion tokens. It activates only 3B of 30B parameters per token and achieves state-of-the-art results among fully open models for both languages. This model demonstrates that sovereign, open-source models can outperform larger dense models while being more efficient for long-context deployment, reducing reliance on proprietary AI systems. It sets a new benchmark for European AI sovereignty and open research. The hybrid Mamba-Transformer MoE architecture keeps the inference cache nearly constant as context grows, giving a throughput advantage over dense models. Soofi S outperforms Olmo 3 32B and Apertus 70B on English and German benchmarks, and achieves the best code aggregates among 17 open base models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models are large neural networks trained on vast text data that can be adapted for various tasks. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, improving efficiency. Mamba is a state-space model architecture that offers efficient long-context processing, while Transformers are the dominant architecture for language models. Soofi S combines both in a hybrid design.

PrismML released Bonsai 27B, a 27-billion-parameter model that can run on a phone via novel quantization techniques, reducing memory from 50GB to 4GB. This breakthrough enables large language models to run locally on mobile devices, potentially transforming on-device AI capabilities and privacy, though its practical advantage over smaller models is debated. The model uses ternary quantization to achieve extreme compression, but community tests show it underperforms compared to smaller models like Qwen 3.5 4B and Gemma 4 12B in some tasks, especially tool calling.

## [AI Coding Enables Tower Without Shared Understanding](https://lucumr.pocoo.org/2026/7/13/the-tower-keeps-rising/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Armin Ronacher argues that AI-assisted coding allows software construction to continue even after shared understanding among developers has collapsed, creating a 'tower' that rises without structural integrity. This challenges the traditional view that large software projects require coordinated understanding, and warns that AI-generated code may lead to unmaintainable systems that are hard to debug or extend. The essay draws a parallel to the Lisp Curse, where ease of individual creation discourages collaboration, and uses the Tower of Babel metaphor to illustrate how AI removes the immediate failure signal of miscommunication.

**Background**: The Lisp Curse describes how Lisp's flexibility makes it easy for individuals to build custom solutions, reducing incentive to create reusable, general-purpose libraries. The Tower of Babel story illustrates how loss of common language halts construction; here, AI allows construction to continue despite lost understanding.

This analysis provides concrete data to debunk assumptions about Wayland's latency, helping gamers and developers make informed choices about display servers and configurations on Linux. The test used a 500Hz display, which may mask frame-bound latency issues that would be more apparent at 60Hz or 120Hz; the XWayland result was about 3ms slower than native Wayland.

**Background**: Input latency is the delay between a user action (e.g., mouse click) and the corresponding visual feedback on screen. X11 and Wayland are competing display server protocols on Linux, with Wayland designed to be more modern and secure. VRR (Variable Refresh Rate) synchronizes the display's refresh rate with the GPU's frame output to reduce tearing. DXVK translates DirectX calls to Vulkan, enabling Windows games on Linux.

## [Message Format Effects in Multi-Hop Agent Relays Are Tier-Dependent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09678) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study introduces a controlled relay testbed to measure how message format affects information fidelity across multiple hops in LLM agent chains, finding that format effects depend on the relay's capability tier. This research challenges existing literature by showing that structured messages do not universally degrade fidelity; instead, format choice should be guided by the weakest relay in the pipeline, which has direct implications for designing robust multi-agent systems. The study tested five formats (free NL, precision-instructed NL, JSON, triples, key-value) over six hops with two relay tiers, finding that under a weak relay the across-format spread of recall grew by a factor of 8.7, driven by an encoding toll and drift resistance specific to JSON schema.

## [YUKTI: Robust Decision-Making from Natural Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09706) ⭐️ 8.0/10

YUKTI introduces a typed-proposition graph with uncertainty and provenance to generate robust, verifiable decisions from natural language, replacing fragile single-objective optimization with Assumption-Robust Pareto Frontiers and regret certificates. This framework addresses the fragility of current language-model-based optimization pipelines by providing theoretical guarantees and reducing regret by over 90% in controlled tests, potentially transforming AI-driven decision-making in high-stakes domains like healthcare and finance. YUKTI uses distributional Pareto hand-off between stages, resamples assumptions via structural epsilon-contamination, and proves a bound making the survival score an exact factor of decision regret; it also includes a benchmark generation tool called SRJANA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Current natural-language-to-optimization pipelines (e.g., NL4Opt, OptiMUS) commit to a single objective and point-valued coefficients, then solve once, making them fragile to misspecification. YUKTI instead models uncertainty in coefficients and objectives, using Pareto frontiers to explore trade-offs and regret certificates to quantify robustness.

<a id="item-29"></a>
## [Epistemic State Replication: Belief Over Bits for Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09748) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Epistemic State Replication (ESR), a novel belief-replication layer for agentic distributed systems that shifts replication from bitwise state agreement to semantic belief agreement. It formalizes the epistemic node state as a pair separating deterministic evidence from stochastic belief lineage, and defines new consistency models like Semantic Linearizability and Bounded Eventual Coherence. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of classical State Machine Replication (SMR) in stochastic, model-driven agentic systems, enabling replicas to make semantically equivalent decisions without forcing identical bitwise states. It could significantly improve flexibility, reduce context amnesia, and enhance performance in distributed AI agent coordination. ESR separates the node state into an immutable evidence log (L) and a stochastic belief lineage (B). It introduces Semantic Linearizability, requiring operations to reflect the latest committed operational meaning within a verifier-bounded semantic compatibility metric, and Bounded Eventual Coherence, which bounds expected semantic divergence under specific assumptions. The paper also formalizes Verifiable Semantic Rollbacks to prune faulty premises without context amnesia.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Classical State Machine Replication (SMR) assumes deterministic transitions to achieve identical bitwise states across replicas, which works well for traditional distributed systems. However, agentic systems using generative models (e.g., LLMs) are stochastic and may produce different reasoning paths yet reach semantically equivalent decisions. Forcing bitwise agreement in such systems degrades flexibility and performance, motivating the need for belief-based replication.

<a id="item-41"></a>
## [LLMs Fail to Seek Critical Data in Clinical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10275) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that large language models (LLMs) achieve only 68% accuracy in an agentic clinical reasoning task in hematologic oncology, despite scoring high on reasoning rubrics. The models fail to proactively request critical diagnostic data, with information utilization dropping from 57% to 26% in the final round. This finding highlights a critical safety gap for deploying LLMs in healthcare: models may appear competent on static benchmarks but fail to gather necessary information under uncertainty. The study underscores that the primary limitation is not medical knowledge but systematic information-seeking failures, which could lead to misdiagnosis in real clinical settings. The study evaluated 32 frontier models across three sequential rounds of data requests before diagnosis and treatment planning. Error analysis identified search satisficing, anchoring, and premature closure as dominant failure modes—cognitive biases also seen in novice clinicians.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical reasoning involves actively deciding what information to gather under uncertainty, unlike static medical knowledge assessments. The study used an agentic evaluation framework where models had to request clinical data proactively, mimicking real-world diagnostic processes. Information utilization—the fraction of available data actually requested—was the strongest predictor of accuracy.

**Tags**: `#large language models`, `#clinical reasoning`, `#AI safety`, `#medical AI`, `#agentic evaluation`

## [TradeLens: Can LLM Trading Agents Pay Their Own Costs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10286) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce TradeLens, a trace-grounded diagnostic toolkit that evaluates whether LLM-based trading agents generate enough profit to cover their operational costs, shifting focus from performance metrics to economic viability. This work addresses a critical gap in current evaluations of LLM agents by introducing economic viability as a key criterion, which could influence the design and deployment of agentic systems in finance and beyond. TradeLens reconstructs trading trajectories, attributes profit and cost to interpretable evidence, and diagnoses failure patterns like poor asset selection in DeepSeek-V3.2 and negative timing in GLM-4.7, showing that capital scale and trading frequency only amplify decision-attributed timing value.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as agents in trading systems, where they make decisions, use tools, and incur costs. ## [SPARK: Steering LLM Reasoning via Hidden-State Susceptibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10296) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced SPARK, a method that uses length-controlled hidden-state susceptibility to diagnose and steer latent reasoning states in LLMs, enabling targeted test-time interventions that improve accuracy on math benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation of output-level evaluation by providing a way to diagnose why a model fails internally, and it demonstrates that susceptibility can serve as a practical guide for lightweight steering, potentially improving reasoning in deployed LLMs without retraining. SPARK uses length-controlled susceptibility to separate input-scale effects from residual reasoning activation, and combines this with cross-layer coordination to select reasoning-active anchors and under-activated hard examples. On MATH-500, it improved Qwen3-4B accuracy from 82.0% to 84.6% and Qwen3-8B from 82.4% to 85.6%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often produce incorrect answers, but it is unclear whether the failure is due to missing capability, an unstable reasoning trajectory, or a failure to activate an existing reasoning state. Existing methods typically evaluate at the output level or apply global activation steering without diagnosing which examples need intervention. SPARK introduces a diagnostic signal based on hidden-state susceptibility, which measures how much a model's internal states change in response to small perturbations, to identify reasoning failures and guide targeted steering.

Co4ICF introduces a co-evolving framework that iteratively fine-tunes a physics-informed surrogate model using trajectories from a PPO-based pulse optimizer, preventing out-of-distribution failures in inertial confinement fusion simulations. ## [Laguerre Geometry Reveals Concept Regions in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10578) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces Laguerre geometry to model concepts in large language models as regions (Laguerre-Voronoi cells) rather than points, and decomposes transformer layers into piecewise-linear flows. The authors propose Geometric Lens, a training-free method to read out the exact concept a hidden vector encodes at any layer. This work provides a precise geometric framework for mechanistic interpretability, potentially enabling better understanding and control of LLM reasoning. It moves beyond point-based representations to capture finer-grained concept structures like hierarchy and inclusion. The framework defines concepts as Laguerre-Voronoi cells or unions thereof, with weights revealing hierarchical structures. The Geometric Lens method is training-free and hyperparameter-free, and the Laguerre Autoencoder provides a 2D visualization of decision geometry and reasoning trajectories.

**Background**: Large language models represent concepts in high-dimensional hidden states, but existing interpretations often treat concepts as points or linear directions, which may oversimplify the geometry. The transformer architecture processes tokens through layers of attention and feed-forward networks, which the paper decomposes into piecewise-linear operators.

## [Agentic-DPO: Offline Preference Optimization for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10601) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Agentic-DPO introduces a lightweight offline method that converts expert trajectories into state-conditioned preference supervision, training LLM agents to choose correct actions against plausible mistakes without costly online rollouts or reward models. This method addresses a key limitation of supervised fine-tuning (SFT) for LLM agents, which only imitates expert actions rather than learning to avoid mistakes, and it achieves competitive performance with online methods like GRPO at a fraction of the cost. Agentic-DPO uses Policy-Preserving Augmentation (PPA) to avoid mixing policy and schema in preference learning, and it improved tau-bench accuracy from 21.7% (SFT) to 41.4% for a 9B model, matching online GRPO without environment interaction during gradient steps.

**Background**: LLM agents are typically trained via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on expert trajectories, which treats multi-turn behavior as text imitation but fails to teach agents to avoid plausible mistakes. Preference learning and reinforcement learning can address this but often require expensive online rollouts and reward models. Agentic-DPO is an offline alternative that leverages DPO-style preference optimization on expert trajectories.

## [E-P-R Framework Diagnoses AI Agent Memory Consumption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10608) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the Entry-Propagation-Recovery (E-P-R) framework to diagnose how AI agents consume conflicting memory across multi-step actions, and introduce MemTrapBench to isolate these phases. This work shifts focus from memory storage and retrieval to consumption, revealing a 'compliance trap' where conflicting memory causes stronger agents to suffer larger performance drops, highlighting the need for safe memory usage in long-horizon tasks. The E-P-R framework identifies three phases: Entry (where memory first changes an action), Propagation (whether that change carries forward), and Recovery (whether the agent can recover after divergence). Experiments on WebArena and MemTrapBench show that main failures begin at entry, and repeated exposure amplifies early errors.

**Background**: AI agents increasingly use memory to reuse past experience in long-horizon tasks like web browsing. Existing research focuses on what to store and retrieve, but overlooks how models consume retrieved memory across multiple steps. DEGS defines a per-sequence collapse depth D(x) and combines it with sequence likelihood in an MCMC power-sampling framework (DEGS-MCMC). It trails an in-house GRPO reference on math splits but surpasses it out-of-domain on GPQA for all three tested models.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often use reinforcement learning (RL) to improve reasoning, but RL requires expensive training, curated data, and reward signals. Recent test-time sampling methods sharpen base-model distributions but rely only on output-layer likelihoods, ignoring internal transformer dynamics. DEGS exploits the observation that stronger reasoners exhibit a 'late collapse' in layer-wise entropy, using this as a signal to guide sampling.

Experiments on reasoning benchmarks show ARMOR effectively mitigates validation collapse and enables sustained improvements over extended training.

The framework is evaluated on FiveQubit, Steane, Planar3x3, and Planar5x5 codes across five regimes, with Meta-MLP achieving teacher-label accuracies up to 0.9993 and VQC up to 0.9400, but logical-level evaluation shows confidence-gated fallback reduces failure ratios significantly.

The paper introduces the Distributed Agent System (DAS), a device-edge-cloud framework that enables fault-tolerant collaboration among heterogeneous embodied agents. ## [Deep Equilibrium Models Can Be Silent No-Ops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11116) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper shows that in port-Hamiltonian deep equilibrium models with learned initialization, the solver is effectively bypassed: the equilibrium equals the initial point to numerical precision, and removing the solver does not change accuracy. This finding challenges the core promise of deep equilibrium models—that they perform input-adaptive iterative inference—and reveals a critical failure mode that could undermine trust in implicit neural networks for reasoning tasks. Across 19 training runs on ProofWriter and graph-reachability tasks, bypassing the solver changed test accuracy by +0.00 percentage points in 18 runs; iteration counts showed no correlation with ground-truth difficulty (r=0.009), and the full model never outperformed a two-layer MLP.

EAST requires LLM-LLM dyads to independently converge on semantic Schelling points under varying epistemic transparency; coordination failures primarily stem from conflating private knowledge with mutual knowledge, even in models that perform well on static benchmarks.

**Background**: Theory of Mind (ToM) is the ability to attribute mental states to others, crucial for social interaction. Traditional ToM tests like the Sally-Anne task are often used to evaluate LLMs but may be compromised by memorization. The Schelling point concept, from game theory, describes a natural focal point that people choose to coordinate without communication. EAST combines these ideas to create a more robust evaluation of functional ToM.

## [Harness-Native Agentic Routing: A New Paradigm for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11399) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes Harness-Native agentic routing, a step-level routing paradigm that selects models based on the full execution state and intermediate failures, rather than single-turn cost-quality trade-offs. It introduces OpenSquilla, an open implementation with a four-layer routing stack and a LightGBM cold-start ranker. This paradigm shift addresses a core systems problem in LLM agents, where model selection must consider execution state and feedback loops. It could significantly improve cost-effectiveness and accuracy of agentic systems, and the data flywheel effect enables continuous improvement. The routing decisions produce structured data records (query, state, model choice, trace, outcome, cost) with labels from the environment, forming a harness-native data flywheel. The paper evaluates on agentic benchmarks DRACO and PinchBench, studying both singleton and multi-model routing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model agents are executed by a harness managing observation, context, control, action, state, and verification. Frontier and open models are becoming structurally specialized, making model selection a core systems problem. Existing routing methods mostly optimize single-turn cost-quality trade-offs, missing the execution state and feedback loops unique to agents.

## [LASKO: Lie Algebroid Framework for Agentic Skill Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11493) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LASKO introduces a novel framework that models agentic skill edits using Lie algebroids, capturing non-commutativity and structured dependencies, achieving up to 15x speedup in preliminary benchmarks. This work provides a rigorous mathematical foundation for optimizing skill edits in agentic systems, potentially enabling more efficient and reliable self-improvement in AI agents. The framework uses a controlled Lie algebroid with anchor ρ to map edit policies to visible Markdown effects, with the kernel representing latent structure and the bracket measuring non-commuting compositions. Preliminary results show a 15x speedup on a causal extraction task compared to brute-force validation with a 671B parameter model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Lie algebroids are mathematical structures that generalize Lie algebras, consisting of a vector bundle with a Lie bracket on its sections and an anchor map to the tangent bundle. They are used to model infinitesimal symmetries and have applications in differential geometry and mechanics. In this context, they provide a way to handle non-commutative and structured skill edits in agentic systems.

## [Robot-Centric Pointmaps Bridge Camera-Action Gap in VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11498) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose robot-centric pointmaps, a 3D representation that stores scene point coordinates in the robot's frame, to resolve the frame mismatch between camera observations and robot actions in vision-language-action (VLA) models. This method improves generalization across viewpoints on RoboCasa and in real-robot experiments. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in VLA models that hinders generalization across different camera setups, enabling more robust robot learning from diverse demonstration data. By integrating seamlessly with existing 2D VLA architectures, it offers a practical path toward scalable and viewpoint-invariant robot policies. Pointmaps store 3D coordinates in the robot frame while preserving the dense H×W grid format expected by pretrained 2D VLAs, requiring minimal architectural changes. On RoboCasa, pointmaps improve both pi0.5 and SmolVLA, outperforming camera-viewpoint and 3D-aware baselines; real-robot tests show the advantage over RGB-only policies widens when the camera is moved to unseen placements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that directly output robot actions from visual observations and language instructions. They typically use a 2D image as input, but robot actions are defined in the robot's 3D coordinate frame, creating a frame mismatch that becomes problematic when training data includes diverse camera viewpoints. Robot-centric pointmaps provide a way to inject 3D geometry into the 2D image representation without altering the model architecture.

Researchers propose Proxy-guided Update Signal Transfer (PUST), a novel post-training framework that decouples update-signal exploration from distribution alignment using a lightweight proxy model. PUST significantly reduces computational overhead by enabling asynchronous generation, caching, and reuse of optimization signals, and supports weak-to-strong improvement and cross-model transfer, making LLM post-training more modular and cost-efficient. The framework consists of three stages: proxy exploration, update-signal extraction, and signal transfer. Evaluations on Qwen3-family models show that update signals from substantially weaker proxies can robustly enhance stronger primary models in math and code domains.

A new paper establishes a formal equivalence between predictive accuracy and profitability in prediction markets with central limit order books, introducing 'proper' betting strategies that convert accuracy into profit. The proper betting strategy depends only on the forecaster's prediction and the market price, earning positive expected profit whenever the prediction outperforms the price under a strictly proper scoring rule and sufficient liquidity. This paper provides the first non-asymptotic convergence analyses for Q-learning and actor-critic methods in distributionally robust average-reward Markov decision processes, achieving optimal sample complexity of O(ε^{-2}). This work fills a critical theoretical gap in distributionally robust reinforcement learning, enabling provably efficient and robust policy learning under model misspecification, which is essential for safe RL applications. The analysis introduces a novel semi-norm contraction property for the robust Bellman operator, and the actor-critic algorithm learns an ε-optimal robust policy with O(ε^{-2}) sample complexity.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) often assumes the environment model is known or can be learned exactly, but in practice models are often misspecified. Average-reward MDPs consider long-run average reward rather than discounted cumulative reward, which is more suitable for continuing tasks. This paper provides the first theoretical guarantees for model-free robust average-reward RL.

A new study on Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B and LLaVA-1.5-13B reveals that over 90% of lexical scene descriptions in vision-language models change depending on the agentic context, a phenomenon termed 'affordance drift'. This finding challenges the assumption of static world models in AI and suggests that context-dependent affordance computation is a fundamental property of VLMs, with implications for robotics and embodied AI where dynamic, query-driven ontologies may be needed. The study used 3,213 scene-context pairs from COCO-2017 with 7 agentic personas, achieving a mean Jaccard similarity of 0.095 between contexts, and confirmed the effect via stochastic baselines (2,384 runs) and Tucker decomposition revealing latent factors like 'Culinary Manifold' and 'Access Axis'.

**Background**: Affordance refers to the action possibilities offered by an environment or object. Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual information to understand scenes. A new momentum schedule derived from critical damping achieves 2.34x faster convergence to 90% accuracy on ResNet-18/CIFAR-10, and a hybrid recipe eliminates the final accuracy deficit while preserving speedup. This work provides a principled, hyperparameter-free momentum schedule that significantly accelerates training without sacrificing final accuracy, potentially reducing computational costs for deep learning practitioners. Researchers propose IntentVLA, a history-conditioned VLA framework that encodes recent visual observations into a short-horizon intent representation to resolve observation aliasing in robot imitation learning. By improving rollout stability across multiple benchmarks, IntentVLA could enhance the reliability of robot manipulation systems in real-world applications. ## [Gefen: Memory-Efficient Optimizer Cuts AdamW Memory by 8x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13894) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Gefen is a novel optimizer that reduces AdamW's memory footprint by approximately 8x by sharing second-moment estimates across parameter blocks and quantizing the first moment with a learned codebook, achieving a reduction of 6.5 GiB per billion parameters. This breakthrough addresses a critical memory bottleneck in large-scale deep learning, enabling training of larger models or larger batch sizes on the same hardware, and can significantly improve throughput in distributed training. Gefen infers block structure from initial squared gradients without requiring architecture-specific metadata, and uses a histogram-based dynamic programming quantization codebook that is also applied to first-moment scaling.

**Background**: AdamW is a widely used optimizer in deep learning, but it stores two moment buffers (first and second moments) per parameter, doubling the memory footprint. This becomes prohibitive for large models with billions of parameters. Gefen reduces this overhead by sharing second moments across groups of parameters and compressing the first moment via quantization.

## [DemoPSD: Disagreement-Modulated Self-Distillation for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02502) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DemoPSD introduces a disagreement-modulated self-distillation method that steers the student toward a reverse-KL barycenter target, avoiding privileged information leakage in on-policy self-distillation for LLMs. This addresses a fundamental flaw in on-policy self-distillation—privileged information leakage—which can cause overfitting and poor generalization. DemoPSD's theoretical framework and practical method could improve LLM training efficiency and cross-domain reasoning. DemoPSD adaptively blends teacher and student distributions at each token position using a reverse-KL barycenter, achieving leakage attenuation and exploration preservation. Experiments on SciKnowEval show it outperforms GRPO and SDPO while maintaining higher training entropy and robust out-of-distribution generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) trains an LLM by having it act as both teacher and student, where the teacher has access to privileged information (e.g., future tokens). This can cause the student to learn shortcuts based on that privileged info, hurting test-time performance. DemoPSD mitigates this by selectively adopting teacher guidance based on distribution disagreement.

This paper extends the Feedback-Coupled Memory Systems (FCMS) framework to continuous time, defining agent updates via Mechanism-Based Intelligence (MBI) and environmental updates via Coupled Memory Graph Process (CMGP), and proves a Lyapunov global dissipativity condition. This work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for continuous-time memory architectures, unifying discrete and continuous stability conditions and revealing that memory dissipation must outpace feedback gain as a universal organizing principle. The Lyapunov global dissipativity condition is governed by the threshold 4β² < 2ημγ², which generalizes the discrete FCMS condition 4ηβ² < γ and CMGP's bifurcation threshold α_c = 1/K. ## [Cost of Correctness in Weak Multi-Agent Swarms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09765) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a theoretical framework for optimally placing strong oracle correctors in a weak multi-agent swarm to achieve correct consensus under a budget, proving submodularity of the coherence function and deriving budget-correctness frontiers. This work provides a principled way to trade off cost and correctness in distributed consensus systems, which is crucial for deploying reliable swarms with limited resources. The task-dependent cost-quality curvature insight guides whether to invest in few strong or many medium correctors. The coherence measure H(R) = tr M(R)^{-1} is shown to be submodular even with heterogeneous oracle strengths, enabling a greedy algorithm that achieves a (1-1/e) approximation. The budget-correctness frontier B*(ε) is derived in closed form for complete graphs, and a minimal oracle count k* is given for equal-cost oracles.

This paper introduces Looped Mamba and Looped Hybrid Mamba-Transformer architectures, which repeatedly apply a shared Mamba block to introduce recurrent computation, and shows they outperform parameter-matched non-looped baselines on reasoning tasks and language modeling. On Mano (modular arithmetic) and p-hop induction tasks, Looped Mamba matches or exceeds non-looped models of equal effective depth; under iso-parameter and iso-FLOPs protocols, looped models remain competitive with fewer distinct parameters, though deeper non-looped models have an advantage in validation perplexity.

This paper introduces a new classification setting called regulation-driven fine-grained hierarchical classification, where labels must obey explicit regulatory hierarchies and rules. The authors propose a constraint-aware hierarchical search framework that converts regulatory documents into a searchable tree and retrieves only valid candidate nodes, achieving best mean accuracy on four new benchmarks. This work addresses a practical gap in domains like customs tariff classification and export control, where existing methods fail to jointly enforce hierarchical validity, rule consistency, and fine-grained boundary reasoning. Experiments show it recovers ground-truth formulas more accurately than existing methods under uncertainty.

Researchers propose StructAgent, a state-centered framework that uses a unified causal state representation and structured workflow to improve long-horizon task execution in LLM/VLM-based digital agents. This framework addresses a key limitation of current agents—reliable long-horizon execution—by enabling explicit progress tracking, failure recovery, and verification, which could significantly advance autonomous digital agents for real-world tasks. On OSWorld-Verified, StructAgent improves Qwen3.5-9B from 27.0% to 46.9% success rate and Qwen3.5-27B from 31.6% to 62.2%, achieving a new open-source state-of-the-art of 78.9% with MiniMax-M3; it also generalizes to Minecraft environments.

This work addresses a key challenge in omni-modal QA: tracking evidence sufficiency across sparse multimodal sources. Omni-Decision maintains a structured evidence state containing confirmed evidence, unresolved conflicts, fact and computation dependencies, and open evidence needs. Heterogeneous observations from media, web, computation, and verification modules are normalized, judged, and committed through deterministic state updates.

## [SciML Study Shows Structural Priors Can Hurt Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09684) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new diagnostic study evaluates five SciML models on macroeconomic forecasting across 23 countries, finding that less-constrained models like ARIMA and NODE outperform heuristic-prior models like PINN and UDE when structural priors are unreliable. This study challenges the common assumption that more structural priors always improve SciML models, providing empirical evidence that priors can act as misregularizers. It offers a practical hierarchy for practitioners and highlights the need to test whether structure helps before applying it. None of the models achieved consistently strong forecasting performance, but ARIMA and NODE consistently outperformed PINN and UDE, with failure modes including prior misalignment, regime shifts, and optimization instability.

## [DenseAR: Coarse-to-Fine Autoregressive Visual Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09892) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DenseAR introduces a next-dense-stride prediction method for autoregressive image generation, using a single-scale tokenizer to predict multiple tokens in parallel from coarse to fine. It unifies multimodal tasks like cross-modal translation, conditioned generation, and segmentation in a single backbone. This approach improves inference speed by avoiding slow raster-order autoregression and reduces complexity compared to multi-scale methods. It demonstrates strong performance on both medical and natural images, potentially advancing efficient multimodal generative AI. DenseAR uses a compact single-scale tokenizer and traverses a latent grid with progressively denser strides to capture global structure to fine details. On ImageNet, it improves FID and IS over single-grid and multi-scale baselines; on brain MRI, it unifies translation, generation, and segmentation tasks.

**Background**: Autoregressive models generate images pixel by pixel or patch by patch in a fixed order, which can be slow. Multi-scale methods improve quality but require long token sequences. DenseAR addresses both issues by predicting multiple tokens at each stride, enabling efficient coarse-to-fine generation.

This paper introduces Probabilistically Robust Minimax-Regret Equilibrium (PR-MRE), a novel solution concept for adversarial team games under asymmetric information that combines distribution-free robustness with probabilistic information. PR-MRE addresses limitations of Bayesian Nash equilibrium and distributionally robust approaches, offering stronger worst-case guarantees against strategic distribution shifts, which is crucial for multi-agent AI systems in adversarial environments. The paper formulates PR-MRE as a robust bilinear program with a tractable semidefinite relaxation, and implements it in a robust double-oracle framework (PRMRE-PSRO) using deep reinforcement learning best responses.

This work addresses a critical but overlooked issue in AML GNN evaluation—performance degradation in dense neighborhoods—which directly impacts the operational cost and effectiveness of automated money laundering detection. SALT-GNN uses up to 77% fewer parameters than task-specific graph-transformer baselines while improving dense-context F1 score by 3-6 points on HI-Small and HI-Medium, and by 16-20 points on AMLSim-32k-5%. PhenoEmbed is a self-supervised model that learns 256-dimensional embeddings of individual tree crown phenology from UAV multispectral time-series data using contrastive and masked reconstruction objectives. This approach provides a reusable forest crown representation that captures seasonal dynamics, potentially improving tree-level models under seasonal change and advancing remote sensing in ecology. The model was trained on HeideBench, an 18-date UAV multispectral benchmark, and on 5,885 crowns the embeddings show structured organization with the first two principal components explaining 25.1% of variance and median top-1 cosine similarity of 0.946.

**Background**: Tree crowns are challenging for AI because their appearance changes significantly across seasons due to leaf emergence, canopy closure, senescence, and leaf-off conditions. ## [Hierarchical Human-AI Triage Model for Nigerian FinTech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10317) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes a hierarchical human-AI triage model for fraud detection in Nigerian FinTech that uses a calibrated ensemble as a primary filter and routes uncertain or high-stakes transactions to human analysts, achieving a 24.79% gain in fraud recall and reducing the regional performance gap from 19.43 to 2.88 percentage points. This work directly addresses structural inequality in FinTech by neutralizing discrimination laundering from infrastructure-related noise, such as rural network timeouts being misinterpreted as fraud, thereby ensuring rural accounts are not excluded from the digital economy. The model uses a three-tier routing policy with a calibrated ensemble model as the primary filter, routes transactions with epistemic uncertainty (e.g., cold start accounts) to specialist analysts, and high-stakes cases to a senior supervisor, while employing a dynamic shadow price to ration human attention and a random audit mechanism to prevent skill atrophy.

**Background**: In many developing economies, FinTech systems rely on data proxies that can encode structural inequalities, such as network timeouts in rural areas being flagged as fraudulent activity. The paper adopts a 'We Are All Equal' worldview to design a human-AI collaboration that ensures substantive equality of opportunity.

## [ABot-N1: A General Visual Language Navigation Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ABot-N1 introduces a slow-fast architecture that decouples cognition from control, using a slow vision-language reasoner for explicit Chain-of-Thought reasoning and a fast action expert for continuous waypoint generation, achieving state-of-the-art results in urban-scale navigation with a 35.0% improvement in POI arrival rate. This work addresses key limitations of monolithic navigation policies, such as coordinate drift and lack of interpretability, by introducing pixel goals as a universal interface, paving the way for more robust and transparent embodied AI systems. ABot-N1 uses a slow vision-language reasoner to produce pixel goals (image-space anchor points) that serve as a universal interface for tasks like point-goal, object-goal, and instruction-following, while a fast action expert generates continuous waypoints at native control frequency using both textual cues and pixel guidance.

A new Dirichlet-process working memory organizes context by task dependency—using a novelty cache, recurrent state, and recency window—instead of token count, scaling memory with distinct information items. This approach could significantly improve LLM efficiency by reducing memory and computation costs on long, redundant context streams, while maintaining auditable and content-addressable memory. Novelty-gated attention achieves full-attention performance while attending to about half the tokens, and coupling with a state-space summary matches full-attention coupling at reduced cost; the advantage grows with longer contexts.

Researchers propose using a single self-supervised token from object geometry, via a Finite Scalar Quantization (FSQ) point-cloud autoencoder, to replace categorical class labels and pose annotations in 3D scene synthesis. Diagnostic probes show the token recovers fine-category (62.6%), super-category (85.6%), and yaw (52.7 deg) information. This work reduces the need for human annotation in 3D scene synthesis, potentially lowering costs and enabling scalable generation. It also provides insights into how geometric tokens encode semantic and pose information, advancing representation learning. The autoencoder is trained on 3D-FUTURE furniture without labels or pose annotations, using chamfer distance. Swapping the chamfer target from rotated to un-rotated point clouds collapses yaw signal while improving class recovery, demonstrating control over rotation content via training objective.

Researchers propose DiffUE, a method that injects noise into the semantic space of images using diffusion autoencoders to create unlearnable examples, improving the trade-off between image utility and unlearnability. This work addresses a critical privacy issue in AI, as unlearnable examples can protect personal images from unauthorized model training without sacrificing visual quality, offering a more robust defense against advanced relearning attacks. DiffUE operates in the semantic space rather than pixel space, leveraging a diffusion-based autoencoder to modify high-level features. Experiments on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, CelebA-HQ, and ImageNet show improved robustness against adversarial training, image transformations, and compression.

Researchers propose answer-conditioned chain-of-thought (CoT) distillation, where a frontier VLM generates visual explanations conditioned on correct labels, and a 3B-parameter model is fine-tuned via LoRA using only 18–30 labeled images per task. Researchers propose three new heuristic metrics—Reference Abstraction (RA), Summary Abstraction (SA), and Abstraction Ratio (AR)—to measure how much a summary diverges from extractive copying, validated on 100 XSUM documents across four models. This approach addresses a critical trade-off between efficiency and semantic reasoning in social navigation, enabling robots to handle routine tasks reactively while engaging VLM reasoning only in socially sensitive situations, improving both performance and real-time feasibility. ## [Model-Free RL for Extended Mean Field Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11005) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a model-free reinforcement learning framework for continuous-time extended mean field control using deterministic policies and policy gradients on Wasserstein space. It addresses limitations of existing stochastic approaches by avoiding optimization over stochastic kernels, enabling efficient learning in problems where dynamics and rewards depend on joint state-action distributions. The method derives a deterministic policy gradient formula with an advantage-rate function on Wasserstein space, and introduces a continuous-time deep deterministic policy gradient algorithm combining particle approximations and measure-dependent neural networks.

**Background**: Mean field control deals with optimal control of large populations of interacting agents, where the state distribution evolves according to McKean-Vlasov dynamics. ## [SynCLIP: Boosting Synonym Robustness in Open-Vocabulary Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynCLIP introduces a framework with two modules—Semantic-consistent Spatial Attention alignment (SSA) and Spatial Attention Refinement (SAR)—to align spatial attention maps across synonymous expressions, and a Synonym-Enriched Visual Corpus (SEViC) for training. This addresses a critical but overlooked problem in open-vocabulary dense perception: synonym-induced grounding inconsistency, which undermines robustness in real-world applications. SynCLIP achieves state-of-the-art performance among CLIP-based methods, improving reliability when objects are described with diverse linguistic variants. The SSA module minimizes discrepancies between attention maps of original and synonymous expressions, while the SAR module selectively strengthens the most semantically relevant spatial regions. The SEViC dataset augments each category with multiple synonyms and textual definitions to support training.

**Background**: Open-vocabulary dense perception (OVDP) aims to localize objects unseen during training by using textual knowledge. ## [BeatEdit: First Framework for Symbolic Music via Explicit Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11124) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BeatEdit introduces the first symbolic music generation framework based on explicit edit operations, using a beat-grid-anchored representation (BEAT encoding) to enable selective modification instead of generating from scratch. This work fills a gap in symbolic music generation by enabling efficient editing, which is closer to how musicians actually compose. It achieves higher precision and perceptual quality than autoregressive and diffusion methods while being fast (under 100 ms inference). BeatEdit comprises three mechanisms: per-token sequence tagging for error correction, iterative refinement for accompaniment editing, and tag-then-fill for segment completion. All share a single encoding and pre-trained backbone, and cross-encoding evaluation shows encoding design significantly affects editing effectiveness.

## [Pix2Act: 2D Keypoint Trajectories for 3D Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11167) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Pix2Act proposes an imitation learning method that represents manipulation actions as 2D keypoint trajectories in camera planes, enabling equivariant data augmentation and lossless 3D pose recovery via triangulation. This reformulation simplifies high-dimensional 3D control into a more learnable 2D prediction problem, improving generalization and robustness under camera perturbations, which is critical for real-world robotic manipulation. Pix2Act aligns observations and actions in the same coordinate space, allowing equivariant transformations to jointly rotate camera images and their image-space actions, and uses a multi-view fusion architecture that respects per-view rotations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: In computer vision, triangulation determines a 3D point from its projections onto two or more images, requiring known camera parameters. Imitation learning in robotics often struggles with high-dimensional action spaces and limited data. Pix2Act addresses these by predicting 2D trajectories that can be triangulated to 3D poses, leveraging equivariant augmentation to effectively increase data diversity.

## [Structure-Feature Aligned Graph Learning via Constrained Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11577) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new constrained two-view framework for node prediction is introduced, featuring a Channel-Split Adaptive Gated GNN (CSAG-GNN) and a stable cyclic alternating optimization strategy to align structure-conditioned embeddings with structure-free features. This work decouples the dependency between feature transformation and neighborhood aggregation in GNNs, improving robustness to topology noise and heterophily, which is critical for real-world graph data with noisy or diverse connections. The framework uses an independent anchor network to learn a structure-free feature prior via self-supervised reconstruction, and the CSAG-GNN dynamically routes representations between global spectral smoothing and local spatial discrimination using a node-wise gating mechanism.

EmbodiSkill introduces a training-free framework that enables embodied agents to self-evolve skills from task trajectories by distinguishing skill content errors from execution lapses. On ALFWorld, it boosts a frozen Qwen3.5-27B executor to 93.28% task success, outperforming GPT-5.2 as a direct agent by 31.58%. By enabling agents to accumulate reusable procedural knowledge without retraining, EmbodiSkill could accelerate the deployment of adaptable robots in real-world settings. EmbodiSkill uses skill-changing evidence to update the skill body and execution-lapse evidence to preserve valid guidance, ensuring targeted revisions. Experiments on ALFWorld and EmbodiedBench show consistent improvements, with the framework being training-free and compatible with frozen large language model executors.

## [Irrelevant State Training Boosts Generalization in Contextual MDPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper theoretically and empirically shows that training on irrelevant states in contextual MDPs acts as data augmentation, improving generalization to unseen contexts. It proposes Explore-Go, a simple method that adds a pure exploration phase at the start of each training episode to enhance this effect. This insight provides a principled understanding of why irrelevant state training helps generalization, and offers a practical, algorithm-agnostic modification that can be combined with both on- and off-policy RL algorithms. It could lead to more robust zero-shot policy transfer in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous driving. The paper identifies a trade-off: training on irrelevant states improves coverage but can reduce value function accuracy, which hurts generalization. Explore-Go mitigates this by using pure exploration to increase coverage without sacrificing accuracy, and is shown to improve test-time performance across several generalization benchmarks, including partially observable ones.

## [Boltzmann MapReduce Links Statistical Mechanics to Distributed Computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09689) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new paper shows that under local asymptotic normality, the MapReduce reduce operation can be interpreted as a partition function from statistical mechanics, with precision-weighted pooling of worker outputs. This theoretical connection bridges distributed computing and statistical mechanics, potentially leading to new insights for designing efficient and robust distributed algorithms. The inverse temperature β equals the sample size n, and the mode of the resulting partition function corresponds to inverse-variance (precision-weighted) pooling. Frequentist consistency emerges as the zero-temperature limit T=1/n→0.

Researchers propose MMA-Former, a 3D transformer with a novel Window-Specific Mixture-of-Head attention mechanism that dynamically routes entire 3D windows to specialized or common attention heads for adaptive feature extraction without increasing parameters. ## [AutoMatBench: Automatic Optimization for Material Property Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11526) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AutoMatBench extends the MatBench benchmark to evaluate AI models on out-of-distribution (OOD) material data, and introduces a Bayesian optimization toolkit to automatically find optimal benchmarking configurations, reducing cost by over half while achieving similar results. This work addresses a critical gap in material property prediction benchmarking by enabling evaluation on OOD data, which is essential for discovering novel materials. The automatic optimization toolkit reduces computational cost and time, accelerating materials discovery. The study shows that performance discrepancies across different benchmarking configurations are large, and considering causal effects of configurations is necessary. AutoMatBench uses Bayesian optimization to efficiently explore the configuration space, achieving similar results to exhaustive benchmarking with over half the cost saved.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Material property prediction (MPP) uses AI to infer material properties from chemical composition and structure, accelerating materials discovery. MatBench is a widely used benchmark for MPP models, but it only evaluates on in-distribution (ID) data, which limits its ability to assess model performance on novel, out-of-distribution (OOD) materials. AutoMatBench combines MatBench pipelines with OOD evaluation methods to create a more comprehensive benchmarking framework.

Researchers propose a severity-aware curriculum learning framework that trains five large language models sequentially on mild, moderate, and critical medical cases, then selects the best response via BERTScore. This approach improves the consistency and relevance of medical text generation across varying case severities, which is crucial for telehealth systems that need to provide accurate information for both simple and complex queries. The framework uses a three-stage curriculum learning strategy and five independently trained LLMs; during inference, the response with the highest BERTScore is selected, achieving 90.30% after fine-tuning on the MAQA dataset.

## [IRFE-ECG: Continual ECG Deployment Without Replay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01674) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose IRFE-ECG, a method for continual ECG deployment that freezes a pretrained backbone and adds isolated linear experts for each new source domain, using a lightweight router with margin-based fusion to infer source identity without raw ECG replay. This work addresses a practical constraint in multi-source ECG deployment where raw data cannot be retained, offering a memory-efficient alternative that maintains competitive performance while identifying autonomous source inference as the key remaining bottleneck. IRFE-ECG uses frozen 1024-dimensional ECGFounder features, balanced-softmax linear experts per domain, and a validation-calibrated margin rule for top-2 expert fusion; experiments on four datasets show a small gap (0.0111–0.0133 Macro-F1) between top-2 fusion and oracle source-aware selection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to adapt models to new data without forgetting previous knowledge. In ECG analysis, models often encounter data from multiple sources (e.g., different hospitals), but privacy or storage constraints may prevent retaining raw signals for replay. IRFE-ECG builds on frozen features and isolated classifiers to avoid parameter interference, while a router attempts to infer the source domain from features alone.

**Tags**: `#continual learning`, `#ECG`, `#domain adaptation`, `#biomedical ML`

## [FRO: Boosting Adversarial Transferability via Block Stretch and Shrink](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17688) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose FRO, a frontend response-oriented input transformation method that enhances adversarial transferability by applying block-wise stretch-and-shrink and perspective projection to generate diverse transformed views. This work offers a fresh perspective on adversarial transferability by focusing on model frontend responses, potentially leading to more effective black-box attacks against deep learning models. FRO consists of two complementary operators: Local Scaling Operator perturbs local content sampling via block-wise stretch-and-shrink, and Projection Operator modifies global spatial organization through coherent perspective deformation. Experiments on ImageNet show consistent improvements across CNN and Vision Transformer models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 14, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial transferability refers to the phenomenon where adversarial examples crafted for one model can fool another model. Input transformation-based attacks improve transferability by aggregating gradients over transformed inputs, but existing analyses overlook the role of model frontend responses. FRO addresses this gap by treating each transformation as a distinct frontend response for gradient aggregation.

A new multi-model framework for abstractive text summarization is proposed, which integrates multiple fine-tuned transformer models and selects the best summary using automatic evaluation metrics like BERTScore.
